## 2026-10-30 — S2 review step review (orchestrator)

**Verdict**: PASS, no rework. The review step (held or quarantined rows reviewed at the first roster shift start at or after `ingested_at`; a fixed outcome per rule id: VR-010, VR-012 and VR-009 rejected, the others released; any reject wins; values kept) and VR-012 are in, approved by the author on 2026-10-29.
- Orchestrator runs: `npm run check` → 59 files, 3350 passed, 1 todo; `check_all --manual: PASS`; protected paths clean. Ledger: A213 (P), A214 (A), A215 (A); sources S123 to S132, all `agent-read` (or `snippet` where only an excerpt was seen). S132 (Nisga'a guideline) and S126 (Westgard "QC - the Practice") carry the URLs and quotes the fact-check confirmed.
- Fact-check before the executor started: every claim confirmed with caveats that the brief carried (S015 asks for recollection and never says "rejected"; Northern Health backs VR-009 through its label-discrepancy row only and is not independent of the Nisga'a guideline; CLIA 493.1282(b)(2) asks for evaluation, not re-analysis; 21 CFR 211.110(c) gives an example, not a schedule).
- Rulings: every architecture records the run QC outcome (no flag owns it, as for containers in S2c1); C's 298 VR-010 rejections are the approved outcome; the as-of read orders versions by version number before `available_at`, so a version released at review after its successor never overtakes it (data_model section 5 and spec section 3, rule 5, "the latest result version").
- Experiment 1 after the change (reported, never gated): C's exact reconstruction 0.973 (was 0.977; the 22 reads that fell back to a failed first attempt are gone), C's availability 0.905; other architectures unchanged; the sensitivity variants (review at shift end or next day 08:00; VR-015 and VR-020 rejected) give the same table because in this scenario every production hold is a reject or the rule does not fire.

**Open**: a metric for results used although their sample exceeded its stability limit (truth-side), so that the cost of A and B using stale results shows beside C's rejections; it needs a design entry in spec section 8 first.

## 2026-10-30 — S2c3: experiment 2, closed loop (SC8) (executor, uncommitted)

**Result**: experiment 2 runs. The pH probe model (drift, deadband controller, readjustment onto an offline value) is in the feedback path of L1 and off by default, the closed-loop assembly runs the oracle, A, B, C and one sensitivity variant over a seed list written first (30 neutral seeds, `scenarios/experiments/experiment2.json`), and the report validates against a new schema. Every test was seen failing for its stated reason and then passing; base commit e5f4eeb, nothing committed, no ADR touched; `tests/golden`, `sql`, `reference` and `docs/adr` unchanged. The full run (30 seeds, 5 arms, 720 reactor pairs) took 4.9 s plus 0.07 s for the report; the report is written outside the repository, is not committed and is byte-identical across two runs. The container question ("stop and ask" in the brief) is answered by the design, see Choice 1 (approved by the orchestrator).

Slots (8640 per arm over the 720 reactor runs, 12 per run on average; summed over reactors and seeds): oracle readjusted 7535, no new value 1105; A and B readjusted 7405, skipped for a known bias 129, no new value 1106; C readjusted 7288, skipped for a known bias 126, no new value 1226; the modelled variant readjusted 7414, no new value 1226. Offline pH values held per architecture arm: 11610, of which 343 (3.0 percent) had a hold beyond the validity of their stability effect (A046) and are extrapolated (spec section 8, rule 7; the report carries the warning). A and B have the same medians and paired means because they read the same rows and apply the same half of the rule (their intervals differ only by the keyed bootstrap stream).

**Diagnosis, no tuning** (scratch script, not in the repository; same 30 seeds, after rework round 1; a model-internal effect under the assumptions of the result text, not a prediction). With the hold limit (A046) made unreachable, so that VR-010 rejects nothing and no value is excluded for its hold, C's paired differences are 0.00456 (offset), 0.00464 (excursion) and 0.03001 (largest excursion), the same as A's and B's 0.00455, 0.00464 and 0.03001; C then skips 129 values, A and B 129, all for an open container. In this scenario the difference between C and A or B therefore comes mostly from the review step: C's VR-010 rejects a value whose hold exceeds A046 (A213), so the long-hold values (the extrapolated ones, which carry the closed-sample drift of A043 over hours) never reach its decision. The container half of the rule acts alike in A, B and C on the samples a technique error (A064) leaves open (about 2 percent), and the hold half never fires in C, because a row with such a hold is already rejected and is not in the record the arm reads.

**Choices and deviations** (for review; all simulator choices are grade A)
1. Container of a pH sample. The design settles it: spec 2.2 and A037 (closed syringe for pH), the bioreactor profile `containers.ph: closed`, and the technique error of A064 (open cup, extra degassing) as the way a pH sample is open; `inject/types.ts` places the technique error in the truth world and experiment 2. So a pH sample is closed unless a keyed draw per sample (key `(seed, sample, -, 0, error:technique_error)`, probability A064, 0.02 for the experienced staff profile) leaves it open; I did not stop. If the all-in-one analyzer's cup was meant to be the default container (every sample open, A041 on every sample), the setting is `technique_error_probability` = 1 and a test covers that case.
2. Superseded by rework round 1 (ruling R1): one rule for every non-oracle arm, each on the fields its record holds; A and B apply the container half, C both halves.
3. Probe and controller. Tick order in `data_model.md` section 7; the controller returns the reading to the set point when it leaves the deadband (S110); the probe starts with no offset; the drift direction is one keyed draw per reactor; a readjustment sets the probe to read the value at the instant it is made (offset = value - true pH). The oracle readjusts onto the true pH at the draw of the newest sample with a result, on the same lab timing (not onto the true pH at the readjustment instant), so the zero-bias identity is exact.
4. The lab run is shared and open loop (its pH values are placeholders); the pH values of each arm's world exist as the loop series and the arm's offline values, not as a per-arm event log. The recorded structure (states, `available_at`, container, hold context) is computed once per architecture and is shown not to depend on pH values (test). `run.ts` is untouched (outside the brief); its refusal message still says "not built in S1b".
5. A and B (overwrite on a rerun) show a rerun's row from the rerun's measurement; the earlier attempt cannot be recovered from the final table. `holdContext` measures the hold to the first run, so for a rerun row C understates the hold of the value.
6. Structure-only edits: `checkSeeds` extracted from `config.ts` (used by the configs of experiments 1 and 2) and `qualificationsOf` extracted from `from_run.ts`; the experiment 1 tests are unchanged and green.
7. Existing tests changed: `scenarios_data.test.ts` (the scenario list gains SC8; one assertion that SC8 changes only the feedback flag and adds the five settings); `support/eval_schemas.ts` (loads the new schema).
8. The runner is in `engine/tests/support/` (as in S2c2); no `npm run` script.

1. Answered: approved.
2. Answered (R1, below).
3. Answered: approved, worded as a model-internal effect under assumptions (spec section 6.2, Notes from S2c3).

**Rework round 1** (orchestrator verdict REWORK; rulings: Q1 approved, Q3 approved; R1 and R2 below). This round supersedes Choice 2, the context rule of the first build and the A and B rows of the first summary table.
- R1, one arm rule. `decideReadjust` (`engine/src/experiments/arms/closed_loop.ts`) now excludes the newest value when its recorded container is open (every architecture records the container, S2c1) or when its recorded hold time is stored and above A046 (only where `two_clocks` stores `sampled_at`, that is C); A field the record lacks marks nothing. The modelled variant subtracts the A041 default from an open-container value in every arm; a hold above A046 is still excluded. The arm reads the two fields itself (`recordedContext`), because `holdContext` needs both. Hand-typed tests (`closed_loop/arms.test.ts`, A, B and C with the flags of the presets): an open-container value is excluded by A, B and C alike; a hold of 2000 s above the limit of 1800 s is excluded by C only, and A and B readjust onto 7.020; a record that names no container marks nothing. Pipeline tests (`loop.test.ts`): with every sample open (technique probability 1) A, B and C all skip and none readjusts; with none open A and B skip nothing.
- R1 evidence: red against the old rule, run in the scratch copy with the new tests: `Tests 4 failed` (`A: expected { Object (action, onto, ...) } to deeply equal { action: 'skip', ...(3) }`, `expected 7.06 to be close to 7.02`, `a_manual: expected 0 to be greater than 0`); green `Test Files 5 passed (5)`, `Tests 75 passed (75)`. Mutation in a scratch copy: A counts the container only where a hold time is stored (the old behaviour) -> 4 fail in `arms.test.ts` and `loop.test.ts`; C ignores the recorded container -> 6 fail. Done command: `npm run check` -> `Test Files 65 passed (65)`, `Tests 3445 passed | 1 todo (3446)`; `uv run pytest -q` -> `186 passed, 62 deselected`; `check_docs --all` -> `364 files, 0 failure(s)`; `check_all --manual: PASS`.
- R2, docs and re-run. Spec section 6.2, "Notes from S2c3", now states the arm rule and the first reading of the run; `data_model.md` section 7 has the row "Arm rule (context and exclusion)". The full re-run (30 seeds, 5 arms) took 5.4 s; the summary table, the slot counts and the diagnosis above are from it. Changes against the first build: A and B now skip 129 slots for an open container (readjusted 7405), their paired differences moved from 0.00446 to 0.00455 (offset), 0.00455 to 0.00464 (excursion) and 0.02984 to 0.03001 (largest excursion); the oracle, C and the modelled variant are unchanged.
- Observation, open (not a finding of the rework). Of about 50 full runs of the seed list, 2 wrote a report that differed from all the others in one arm and one seed (the first in B at seed 5231, the second in the oracle at seed 5113; counts equal). It did not recur in 36 later runs: 16 parallel full runs with a hash of the shared lab log and of every arm's outcome per seed (all identical), 24 parallel single-seed processes, 25 repeated calls in each of 12 processes, and a run with the optimising compilers off (identical to the reference). `engine/src` has no clock or random source (lint and grep), and the lab hash was the same in every later run. Cause not found; the table above is the result that every later run reproduced. A file changing under a running process (another session was running its own checks in the same tree) is a possibility I could not confirm or exclude.
- Paths added to the behaviour list: `engine/src/experiments/arms/closed_loop.ts` (changed), `engine/tests/closed_loop/{arms,loop}.test.ts` (changed), `docs/design/{data_model,simulation_spec}.md`, `docs/progress.md`.

## 2026-10-30 — S2c3 review (orchestrator)

**Verdict**: PASS after one rework round. Experiment 2 (SC8, closed loop) runs 30 seeds x 5 arms in about 5 s; feedback is off in every other scenario and the S1 goldens stay byte-identical; arms share every draw; a zero-bias configuration gives exactly zero paired difference; the arms zone holds.
- Orchestrator runs: `npm run check` → 65 files, 3445 passed, 1 todo; `check_all --manual: PASS`; protected paths and `eslint.config.js` clean.
- Rulings: pH samples use the closed syringe of A037 and a technique error (A064) can leave one open; one decision rule for every non-oracle arm, each on the fields its record holds (an open container is excluded by A, B and C; a hold above A046 only where the hold time is stored); the modelled variant subtracts A041 in every arm; the diagnosis is kept as a model-internal effect: in this scenario the difference between C and A/B comes mostly from the VR-010 rejections (A213), and with the hold limit disabled C is close to A/B. Spec section 6.2 "Notes from S2c3" carries the rule and the diagnosis.
- Determinism: the executor saw 2 of about 50 full runs differ (B seed 5231, oracle seed 5113) and could not reproduce it. The orchestrator ran the experiment 2 runner 16 times (8 sequential, 8 concurrent processes): all 16 reports have the same sha256; `engine/src` holds no module-level mutable state (two constant sets only). The likeliest cause is runs made while the executor was editing files; not proven. Open for the `sim-adversary` pass at the end of S2, and a two-process determinism test for the experiment runners is Parked for S2d.

**Choices and deviations** (for review; the design points are in the data_model table)
1. Two new scenario settings, `controller_log_cut` and `bay_controllers`, both layer-on only; no base scenario file changed, and a layer-on scenario with no `bay_controllers` stays analyzer-side only.
2. A daily file's `received_at` is the end of its UTC day, (day + 1) x 86400 s; a per-row file's is its stamp.
3. A log with no row has no file (not "banner and header only"); the second family's user-event and error exports are never written.
4. On `adapter_ingest` the historian of the dataset is the logged rows (`historian.late`, canonical tag, `raw_ref`), and the columnar series is not kept; on `manual_transcription` it is the series, as with the layer off. The `ctx_pv_*` columns of the export therefore differ by path.
5. New parse-error code `unknown_name`; `canonical_record.schema.json` widened (null) for the second family; `RawTables` gains `alarm_event` and `batch_phase_record`.
6. The roles of the alarm and phase machines are a stand-in: `rule` for what the process causes, `analyst` for what an operator causes (no operator role exists among the five of data_model 6).
7. VR-025: a held row does not advance the instance, the first row seen is accepted, and the previous record is the previous one received (late controller files are Parked).
8. Hand-given transitions enter the assembly as `setup.forced` (alarms and phases), like the other forced modes; the production run passes none.
9. Controller files get a cheap content hash (two 32-bit lanes); the analyzer's `hashString` costs a BigInt step per four characters and a daily controller file is hundreds of kilobytes. The two hashes are not comparable.
10. `fixtureFiles()` in `engine/tests/support/raw_fixtures.ts` lists the three analyzer dialect folders only; the controller fixtures have their own runner.

**Open questions for the orchestrator**
1. Roles: is `analyst` an acceptable stand-in for an operator's acknowledge, shelve, start, hold and restart, or should data_model section 6 get an operator role (a design change)?
2. Empty logs are not written (choice 3). Keep, or write banner and header only?
3. Choice 4: the logged rows replace the columnar series on the adapter path, so the export's `ctx_pv_*` columns read a step-held, deadband-thinned value there. Keep, or keep the series beside the rows?
4. The controller rows are 99 percent of the raw data rows (SC1 seed 1: about 42,200 of 42,600), so the shares of spec 2.4.7 are diluted by clean controller rows. Keep the literal reading (all rows the emitters wrote), or report analyzer and controller rows as two classes?
5. Pre-existing, not from this batch: `engine/tests/assembly/two_process_determinism.test.ts`, experiment 2, fails intermittently at the base commit (2 of 12 runs with my changes stashed; 7 of 60 pairs of direct runs of `determinism_cli.ts experiment2`). The reports differ in the per-seed metrics of `arms[1]`, seed index 0; with `node --no-opt` there were 0 mismatches in 40 pairs. This points at a floating-point difference between interpreted and optimised code in the closed-loop path, which invariant 2 would call a determinism bug. For `sim-adversary`; I did not touch it (truth modules are out of scope). If it fails in your run, run it again.

**Blocked** (research role; nothing invented)
1. The new L1 `sensor_tick` signals of spec 2.4.4 (agitation, gas flows, feed and base pumps, off-gas CO2) and their parameters; A178 has a deadband for agitation only (A172) and none for the feed pump, the individual gas flows or off-gas CO2. The truth modules are untouched; the logger fails naming A178 for a tag with no deadband. Reported in the key as `ctrl_extra_signals`.
2. The generic controller rule that computes OUT: SP and PV are logged, OUT is not (`ctrl_out`).
3. Acknowledge and return-to-normal times: a production run emits no alarm log (`ctrl_alarm_log`); the alarm emitter, parser and VR-025 are tested on hand-given transitions (test-only values, said so in each test).
4. The review outcome of VR-025: a held controller row never reaches `review()` (it reads the findings of result and sample targets only), so nothing throws "no outcome is defined"; the row stays held and its finding stays open, with no release or rejection. A production run holds none (its transitions are legal by construction). Together with VR-005, VR-006, VR-013, VR-024 and VR-026 of S2e1 this is the list for the author.
5. A175 (alarm deadband and delay for pH and DO) is needed only by a limit-based alarm rule, parked in spec 2.4.3; not built.

## 2026-10-30 — S2e2 review (orchestrator)

**Verdict**: PASS after one rework round. Steps 7 and 8 of S2 (raw device layer, controller side) are in as far as the ledger covers them: deadband and maximum-interval logging of PV and SP (RQ9), ISA-88 phases from the observable process events (RQ11), the alarm emitter, parser and VR-025 on hand-given transitions (RQ10), the controller log cut (A217, grade A, no source), the controller side of the capture path, and the Step 8 checklist for the whole raw layer. With `raw_layer` off nothing changed.
- Orchestrator runs: the three Done gates PASS (`npm run check` 89 files, 3899 passed, 1 todo; pytest 210 passed); protected paths, the truth modules, `eslint.config.js` and ADR-0014 unchanged; the ledger diff is A217 only; all 9 controller fixtures start with the banner; the deadband worked example recomputed by hand (5 s cadence, deadband 1, maximum interval 15 s: rows at 0, 10 and 25 s). Mutations by the orchestrator (restored, `cmp` clean): the temperature set point ignores the shift -> 4 fail; a daily file received at the start of its day -> 4 fail; the `.PV` tag dropped from the as-of read -> 0 fail in the first build, 5 of 7 after rework W2. The `raw/` controller modules import observable types only.
- Rulings: Q1 `analyst` stays the stand-in for an operator step, an operator role is Parked (no production run causes one); Q2 a log with no row has no file; Q3 on `adapter_ingest` the historian is the logged rows, so the export's `ctx_pv_*` columns are step-held values (spec section 5 rule 3, RQ9); no section 8 metric reads a historian value, and a later one that does must report RQ9 apart from linkage; Q4 the controller rows are about 99 percent of the raw rows and diluted the clean-row guard of spec 2.4.7 (quarantining every analyzer row would have read about 1 percent), so every metric is also reported per device class; Q5 see Determinism below. Blocked 1 to 5 accepted as written.
- Rework (W1 to W4): per-class metrics (`by_class`, total and denominators unchanged, spec 2.4.7 one sentence); a test that pins the process-value read on the adapter path; the rulings in the data_model S2e2 table; Step 8 R3 rerun with the corrected grep. The W2c mutant (`.SP` stripped like `.PV` in `processSignalOf`) is equivalent: the signal it creates is looked up as `.PV` and finds nothing, so no test can tell it apart.
- Contrast (30 seeds, reported, never gated): with timing held equal (`per_run`, `per_row`) every metric is 0 [0, 0] for A, B and C; with exports at the window end and controller logs cut daily, C's availability at the decision is -0.586 [-0.626, -0.541], as in S2e1: the controller cut moved no metric. Per class, about 405 analyzer rows and 42,194 controller rows per run; every ingest median is 0.
- Determinism (S2c3 open item, diagnosed): the intermittent two-process mismatch of experiment 2 is a miscompile in Node v25.6.1 (V8 14.1.146.11-node.19, darwin arm64): Maglev escape analysis with concurrent OSR lets distinct metric objects share their HeapNumber field boxes, so an object pushed at `engine/src/experiments/assembly/closed_loop_arm.ts:110` takes the values of a later reactor after the push. Nothing in `engine/src` writes those fields after construction. Measured by the orchestrator (post-push snapshot check, 160 runs each): default flags 4 outputs differ and exactly those 4 runs show a changed object; `--no-maglev-escape-analysis` 160 of 160 identical to the majority output, no change. The adversary pass measured 24 of 480 by default and 0 with each of `--no-maglev`, `--no-turbofan`, `--no-maglev-escape-analysis`, `--no-use-osr`, `--no-concurrent-osr`. The truth run, the default scenario hash and experiment 1 showed no mismatch in 160 runs each; that the truth path can never be hit is not proven. The flag changes no byte of the correct output, so the S1 goldens and every reported result stand. Decision of the author, 2026-10-30: run every Node entry point with the flag, keep Node 25, write ADR-0016 (Proposed) with the browser risk of S3 and S4; a V8 bug report is drafted outside the repository and not filed.
- Process: the Step 8 R3 grep of prompts/20 now uses `git grep --untracked`, because the plain `grep -r` also scanned the gitignored private folder and printed one line of it to an executor's terminal (nothing reached a tracked file).

**Open (held by the orchestrator)**: VR-025 joins the review-outcome list for the author (with VR-005, VR-006, VR-013, VR-024, VR-026); Blocked for the research role: the L1 signals beyond A100, the OUT rule, the acknowledge and return-to-normal times; maintenance next: the runtime flag on every Node entry point, a guard that the flag is present, a non-gated canary that measures the miscompile without it, and ADR-0016.

## 2026-10-30 — Maintenance: Node flag on every entry point, ADR-0016 option 2 (executor, uncommitted)

**Result**: every Node process that runs engine code now carries `--no-maglev-escape-analysis` (Vitest workers, the 3 spawning tests, the 2 Python spawns), the 10 CLI scripts refuse to start without it, guard tests fail when any of them lacks it, and the canary is in. Base commit c10d8b4, nothing committed; no ADR, golden, ledger, prompt, `engine/src`, `package.json` or `.nvmrc` touched; with the flag on, the S1 goldens and every other test pass unchanged. Done line: `uv run pytest -q` -> `213 passed, 76 deselected`; `check_docs --all` -> `531 files, 0 failure(s), 0 warning(s)`; `check_all --manual` -> PASS on the final run (its `npm run check`: lint and typecheck clean, `Test Files 90 passed (90)`, `Tests 3913 passed | 1 todo (3914)`, +14 tests and +1 file on the base). Two rulings of the orchestrator shaped the build (chat, 2026-10-30): the two Python spawn files joined the allowed paths (argv lists only, plus a drift check), and the Vitest setting goes into the existing `vite.config.ts` instead of a new file.

**What exists** (no new dependency, no version change)
- `engine/tests/support/node_flags.ts`: the one list, `ENGINE_NODE_FLAGS = ["--no-maglev-escape-analysis"]`, with a comment pointing to ADR-0016.
- `vite.config.ts`: `test.execArgv: [...ENGINE_NODE_FLAGS]` next to the existing `include` and `environment`, both unchanged. Vitest 5.0.5 takes `test.execArgv` (its own type docs: "Pass additional arguments to `node` process when spawning the worker") and its default pool is `forks`, so the flag lands on the command line of each worker; the pool is left at the default. The brief assumed there was no Vitest config; there was this one. A first build with a separate `vitest.config.ts` merging it was dropped on the ruling above (`tsconfig.json` is back to the base).
- `engine/tests/support/require_node_flags.ts`: a module with one effect. If a flag of the list is missing from `process.execArgv` it prints the flag, ADR-0016 and the full command to stderr and exits 2. Each of the 10 `*_cli.ts` files imports it as its first import, so it runs before any engine module is evaluated. No `v8.setFlagsFromString`. The match is the exact dashed form.
- Spawn sites: `engine/tests/assembly/two_process_determinism.test.ts`, `engine/tests/invariants.test.ts`, `engine/tests/invariants_s1b.test.ts` pass `...ENGINE_NODE_FLAGS` (import and the spawn argument list only). `tests/test_data_dictionary.py` and `tests/user_track/test_queries.py` pass the literal flag in their argv list with a one-line comment (Python cannot import the TS list). `git grep` finds no other place that starts Node; the others are `git` (`raw/schemas.test.ts`) and `node_modules/.bin/json2ts` (`schemas.test.ts`, a Node script that runs no engine code, left as is by ruling).
- `engine/tests/node_flags.test.ts` (14 tests): the list holds the literal flag of the brief; the Vitest worker's `process.execArgv` holds it and every flag of the list; a child spawned with the shared flags prints it in its `execArgv`; every `*_cli.ts` found in `engine/tests/support/` (10, listed at run time, so a new CLI is covered) exits 2 and names the flag and ADR-0016 when started bare (plus a sanity test that the CLI list is not empty).
- `tests/test_node_flags.py` (3 tests): reads the list out of the text of `node_flags.ts` and checks, with the `ast` module, that every `["node", ...]` argument list in `tests/` has exactly those flags right after `"node"`; it also checks that both Python spawns are found. About 25 lines of logic, so it is in.
- `engine/tests/support/v8_canary.ts`: the canary, outside the gate (no test and not `check_all` runs it). `node engine/tests/support/v8_canary.ts [runs per flag set = 160] [concurrency = 4]`. Parent and child are the same file (`--child`); the parent loads no engine code; the child repeats the experiment2 branch of `determinism_cli.ts` (second copy of 5 lines, AHA) and does not import `require_node_flags.ts`, since it must run without the flag. Exit 1 when the flagged set differs or fails.

## 2026-10-30 — S2f: fixes from the S2-end adversary pass, F1 to F8 (executor, uncommitted)

**Result**: all eight fixes are in, each red first and then green, every mutation of the brief is caught, and the capture contrast is unchanged. Base commit bd39f46, nothing committed. No ADR, golden, ledger, schema, prompt, truth module or Node flag file touched; with `raw_layer` off nothing changed (the S1 goldens and every earlier test pass unchanged). Done line: see "Gates" below. Note for the orchestrator: `docs/progress.md` already carried uncommitted edits of the maintenance session when I started (two lines of its entry, not mine); I only appended this entry.

**What changed** (one line per item; expected values typed by hand or taken from the spec, never read from the code under test)
- F1, one row per physical analysis. `raw/rows.ts` groups by analyzer, label, attempt, visit and the event instant; the manifest `src` carries the instant (`raw/types.ts`, `Source.t`); the bridge (`ingest/adapters/to_events.ts`) matches the slot by it. Two analyses that a label error gave one label are two rows with their own stamps, and the bridged stream equals the manual one event for event.
- F2, draws keyed by the physical sample. `AnalysisRow.physical_id`; `overlapDraw` and `entryDraw` are keyed by it; `raw/quirks.ts` holds the plain-data map type (`PhysicalIds`, `resultKey`, `physicalOf`); `inject/entry.ts` and `raw/rows.ts` read it, so neither imports truth. `experiments/assembly/physical_ids.ts` builds the map from the stream before the injector and the stream after it (labels changed, stamps moved, replayed results repeated in place, so they line up result by result; any other shape is an error, never a guess); `run_seed.ts` passes it to `rawPath`. A missing id is an error, not a fall-back to the label.
- F3, overlap repeats a chosen row once. `emitExport` takes `previous` (the export just before, without repeats) and `earlier` (all earlier rows, append mode only); a chosen row of `previous` is repeated in the next export of the same instrument and dialect and nowhere else; the row's draw is listed by the export that first writes it and not again. `raw_path.ts` passes `previous = now` and accumulates `earlier`.
- F4, a reject wins. `record/review.ts` reads an open reject finding on the result or its request: a row in a state from ingest (VR-019 quarantine, a hold) that VR-014 also rejects is reviewed and ends `rejected`, no `available_at`; the reject finding stays open as for a row rejected at ingest. `validate.ts` is unchanged in behaviour (the table of data_model section 6 has no `quarantined` to `rejected` edge, so the review does it through `under_review`); a comment says so.
- F5, an overwrite carries its findings. `record/results.ts` drops the stage findings of the replaced version and writes those of the version the row now holds.
- F6, one profile per bay. `scenario.ts` fails the load when `bay_controllers` has not exactly `ceil(reactor_count / bay_size)` entries, naming the setting and both counts. Two test helpers (`support/raw_run.ts`, `support/contrast.ts`) now give a one-bay variant (SMALL) the first profile only; before, SMALL named two profiles for one bay.
- F7, lint zones without bypasses. `eslint.config.js`: a shared extension `(\.[cm]?[jt]s)?` in `ANSWER_KEY`, `HIDDEN_EVENT`, `RAW_KEY`, the flat-file rule and `TRUTH_IN_FOLDER`; `TRUTH_IN_FOLDER` now matches any relative path whose last two segments are `..` or `src` and a truth module (so `../run.js`, `../../src/run.ts`, `../sensors.js`, `./../run.ts` are refused, while `./process.ts` and `../record/process.ts` stay local); new `TRUTH_READER_FOLDERS` (evaluate/ and experiments/assembly/) in the recorded folders (record, ingest, inject, export, raw; arms keep their own older zone). `npx eslint engine` exit 0 before and after. I also gave the `.js` spelling to `ANSWER_KEY` and `HIDDEN_EVENT`, which had the same hole (not named in the brief; their `.js` bypasses are in the planted tests).
- F8, controller side. (a) `controller_series.ts` throws on a second temperature shift of a unit, naming the unit and both instants, for any unit of the stream. (b) `controller_row.ts` runs the idempotency check before the VR-024 return, as the analyzer parser does (`ingest/row.ts`): a replayed file of unresolved rows is a no-op (VR-008); the analyzer side already behaved so (a parity test pins it), so there was nothing to keep separate. (c) `validate.ts` `requestOfTarget` names a request only for a five-part analyzer source event (`analyzer/request/attempt/visit/analyte`), and `evaluate/detection.ts` counts only findings that resolve to a request as flags.
- Design text: three one-line additions to `data_model.md` section 7 (the "Files" and "From parse to recorder" rows of the S2e1 table, the "Several rules on one row" row of the review table). No other design text.

A production run has none (no ledger rate for a replayed or overlapping file); with a rate they would matter. `ingest_conflict.request_id` already holds the request, so resolving the target through it is a small follow-up if you want it; I did not build it (YAGNI).

## 2026-10-30 — Maintenance: check_all summary names failing tests (executor, uncommitted)

**Result**: the summary of `scripts/check_all.sh` (the `--manual` output and the Stop hook's stderr) now keeps Vitest's indented failure lines: ` FAIL  <file> > <suite> > <test>` for a failed test, ` FAIL  <file> [ <file> ]` for a file that did not load, and the count lines ` Test Files`, `      Tests`, `     Errors` when they report a failure. A passing run adds none of Vitest's lines. Base commit 5232a0d, nothing committed; touched `scripts/check_all.sh` (one `grep` pattern and one comment line), `tests/test_check_all.py` (+6 tests, +1 helper, 3 captured shapes) and this entry. No `package.json`, engine file, ADR, ledger or schema touched; no new dependency.

**Cause**: line 55 filtered the collected output with `^(FAIL|WARN)|check_docs|ERR`. Vitest prints its failure lines with one leading space, so the pattern never matched them; the 2026-10-30 Stop-hook run kept only `FAIL  npm run check failed (exit 1)`.

**What Vitest 5.0.5 prints inside `npm run -s check`, output captured** (scratch project outside the repository, symlinked `node_modules`, planted failures; no TTY, so no colour codes)
| Case | Lines that matter |
|---|---|
| a test fails | ` FAIL  engine/tests/bad.test.ts > suite alpha > inner > fails on purpose` (one space, `FAIL`, two spaces, path, ` > ` between suite names); above it the tree (` ❯ file (4 tests \| 1 failed \| 1 todo)`, `       × name 2ms`) and the `⎯⎯ Failed Tests 1 ⎯⎯` rule; below it the message (`AssertionError: ...`) and a code frame |
| a file does not load, a test times out | ` FAIL  engine/tests/importfail.test.ts [ engine/tests/importfail.test.ts ]`; ` FAIL  engine/tests/timeout.test.ts > slow` with `Error: Test timed out in 50ms.`; `⎯⎯ Failed Suites 1 ⎯⎯` |
| counts, failing | ` Test Files  1 failed \| 1 passed (2)`, `      Tests  1 failed \| 3 passed \| 1 todo (5)`, and with an unhandled rejection `     Errors  1 error`, then `   Start at  11:03:03` and `   Duration  89ms (...)` |
| counts, passing | ` RUN  v5.0.5 <dir>`, ` Test Files  1 passed (1)`, `      Tests  1 passed (1)`, `Start at`, `Duration`; no per-test line (the non-TTY reporter lists only failed files) |
| lint error | `<absolute file path>`, then `  1:7  error  'unused' is assigned a value but never used  @typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars`, then `✖ 3 problems (3 errors, 0 warnings)`; exit 1 |
| typecheck error | `engine/src/tsbad.ts(1,14): error TS2322: Type 'string' is not assignable to type 'number'.`; exit 2 |

With `FORCE_COLOR=1` every one of these lines starts with ANSI escape codes (` FAIL ` becomes `ESC[41mESC[1m FAIL ...`), and the pattern would miss them again; the hook environment sets no colour variable (the incident's own lines had none), so this is noted, not handled.

**Red, then green** (`uv run pytest -q tests/test_check_all.py`)
| Step | State | Result |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | six new tests, old filter | `4 failed, 20 passed`: the four tests that look for the failing test's name and the count lines in the `--manual` output and in the hook's stderr (exit 2), each with two captured shapes; the other two (bounded at 41 lines for 100 failures, a passing run prints no Vitest line) are guards that held under the old filter |
| 2 | filter changed to `^(FAIL\|WARN)\|^ +(FAIL \|(Test Files\|Tests\|Errors) .*(failed\|error))\|check_docs\|ERR`, `head -40` kept | `24 passed` |

The new pattern run over live scratch output (a timed-out test, then a passing run) kept ` FAIL  engine/tests/timeout.test.ts > slow`, ` Test Files  1 failed | 1 passed (2)`, `      Tests  1 failed | 1 passed (2)`, and nothing for the passing run.

**Mutation** (scratch copy of `scripts/`, `tests/` and the project files, restored, `diff -r` clean against the repository)
- the old pattern put back: 4 of 24 tests fail (the four above)
- `head -40` dropped: 1 of 24 fails (the bounded test)
- every indented line kept (`^ +`): 5 of 24 fail (the four, plus the passing-run test)

**Rework** (the orchestrator's one round, same brief: R1 lint and type errors, R2 the cap; the same two files, no other path)
- Captured shapes (scratch project, planted errors, output piped): tsc 6.0.3 prints one line per error, `engine/src/ts_a.ts(1,14): error TS2322: Type 'string' is not assignable to type 'number'.`, relative path, no count line, `npm run check` exits 2. eslint 10.13.0 (stylish) prints a blank line, the file path unindented on a line of its own, then `  2:7  error    <message>  <rule>` and `  3:1  warning  <message>  <rule>` lines under it (the level word padded to the longer one), a blank line between files, then `✖ 8 problems (6 errors, 2 warnings)` and an indented `N errors and 0 warnings potentially fixable with the `--fix` option.`; a run with warnings only prints the file, the warning line and `✖ 1 problem (0 errors, 1 warning)` and exits 0.
- Change (`scripts/check_all.sh`): the `grep ... | head -40` became one `awk` pass (no new dependency). Printed without a cap: each step's `FAIL  <step> failed (exit N)`, the `FAIL  Math.random found ...` line, the Vitest count lines, and eslint's `✖` line when it counts at least one error. Counted against a cap of 40, in output order: everything the first round kept (` FAIL ` lines, `FAIL`/`WARN`/`check_docs`/`ERR` lines), tsc's `error TS####:` lines, and eslint's `l:c  error` lines with their file path, printed once before the first error of the file (a file with only warnings never prints its path; warning lines never print). When lines are cut, a last line `(N more lines not shown)` gives the number.
- Numbers: at most 40 detail lines, plus 3 step verdicts, the Math.random line, 3 Vitest count lines, 1 eslint count line and the note, 49 lines, plus the `check_all --manual: FAIL` line or the hook's hint: 50.
- Red then green: with the first-round filter 7 of 31 failed (tsc and eslint, each in the `--manual` output and the hook's stderr; the eslint path-before-its-first-error test; the cap test in both modes), 24 passed; with the new filter 31 passed. The old 100-failure test became the cap test (`--manual` and hook): it finds `npm run check failed`, both count lines and the Math.random line among 100 failed tests, at most 40 ` FAIL ` lines, and the note's number plus the shown lines equals 100. Two guards held before and after: a passing Vitest run and a warnings-only eslint run add nothing.
- Mutation (scratch copy, restored, `diff -r` clean; failures out of 31): no tsc pattern 2; the eslint error rule off 3; warnings kept 3; the path printed on every error line 1; the `✖` line always kept 1; fixed lines capped too 2; no cut note 2; no cap 2. The filter as it was before the first round 11; the first-round filter 7.
- Live check: the new filter over the real captures kept all six eslint error lines with their two file paths and the `✖ 8 problems` line, all three tsc lines, and the Vitest timeout test with its two count lines; the warnings-only and the passing captures gave nothing.

**Gates** (run in this order after the last edit)
- `uv run pytest -q` -> `226 passed, 76 deselected` (base 213, +13: 6 in the first round, 7 in the rework)
- `uv run --frozen python scripts/check_docs.py --all` -> `539 files, 0 failure(s), 0 warning(s)`
- `bash scripts/check_all.sh --manual` -> PASS (printing the `check_docs` line and `check_all --manual: PASS`; its `npm run check` is untouched by this change)

**Not done / found and left** (nothing here was silently dropped)
- Before the rework a lint or typecheck failure of `npm run check` was invisible in the summary (lowercase `error`, which the case-sensitive `ERR` does not match) and the cap cut the verdict and count lines; both are closed by the rework above.
- Not kept: Vitest's message line (`AssertionError: ...`) and code frame, tsc's continuation lines of a long message, eslint's rule-fix hint; the test name, file and line, and the eslint rule name are enough to go to the place.

**Spike, 2026-10-30** (scratch project in the scratchpad; Node v25.6.1, Vite 8.3.5, Preact 11.0.1, headless Chromium 156.0.8078.4, Firefox 157.0 and WebKit 27.2 for the cross-engine probe, all headless and closed; the repository's `package.json`, lockfile and `node_modules` untouched)
| Measure | Minimal page | Lab surface (injector, recorders, review, evaluator, `runOpenLoop`, `buildReport`, raw layer) |
|---|---|---|
| First load, `gzip -c` sum | 21,471 B | 47,329 B (46.2 KB; 15.4 percent of the 300 KB budget); page chunk 6,087 B, Worker 41,015 B, html 227 B |
| Engine run, default scenario, Worker, median of 5 (I12 marks) | 70.7 to 72.5 ms over 5 page loads | 68.2 to 72.6 ms over 3 page loads (budget 1 s); first run of a load 82.6 to 95.5 ms |
| `runOpenLoop`, one seed, Chromium (Firefox; WebKit) | | 3 architectures 217 ms (315; 164); 11 architectures 406 ms (595; 356) |
| `runOpenLoop`, 20 seeds, Chromium (Firefox; WebKit) | | 3 architectures 3.5 s (6.3 s; 3.4 s); 11 architectures 7.0 s (13.9 s; 8.3 s) |
The engine bundle is not near the budget, so no breakdown was made. Vite compiled the Preact JSX with `oxc.jsx` options and no plugin; `tsc` 6.0.3 and the existing `eslint.config.js` accepted the TSX.

**Finding that changes the browser check (needs the author, Open 1 of the note)**: the strict log hash of the default scenario differs between Node and every browser tried on the same machine, for 5 of 5 seeds, and also between the browsers. Seed 1 in Chromium: 248 of 7,211 log lines differ from the golden, in 253 float fields (`drift_before_pct`, `noise`, `value`), largest relative difference 9.3e-15, nothing structural. A probe of eight `Math` functions shows `log`, `cos`, `sin`, `exp`, `atan` differing in the four engines (`sqrt`, `pow` agree), and the engine calls `Math.log`, `Math.cos` (rng) and `Math.exp` (stability, sensors). A hash that rounds non-integers to 6 significant digits was equal in all four engines on 5 of 5 seeds (also at 9 digits; 4 of 5 at 12), and the rounded digest of the metric cells of seed 1 was equal too. The note therefore designs the check in two tiers (strict, rounded) plus five repeats of seed 1 inside the Worker, with the reference built in Node under the ADR-0016 flag and refused unless it equals the golden `log_hash`. ADR-0016's plain "compare with the shipped hash" would show a failure in every browser. The goldens and the engine are untouched.

**Gates** (run after the note and this entry were written; Step 0's "before changing anything" run was not made in that order, the base was green at the S2g review entry)
- `uv run pytest -q` -> `226 passed, 76 deselected`
- `uv run --frozen python scripts/check_docs.py --all` -> `545 files, 0 failure(s), 0 warning(s)`
- `bash scripts/check_all.sh --manual` -> `check_all --manual: PASS`

**Parked**: a deterministic maths port for the five `Math` call sites in `rng.ts`, `stability.ts` and `sensors.ts` (needs the author, golden approval); a self-consistency run for configurations the reference does not cover; SC8 in the lab; a rank column and the review-timing variants on Results; a data-package download; a neon theme in the lab; a hint and a leaderboard in the challenge; Firefox and WebKit as a gate (the optional `engines` check only).

## 2026-10-30 — S3m: deterministic maths (executor, uncommitted)

**Result**: ADR-0019 option 2 is built. `engine/src/xmath.ts` (new, 300 lines) gives `log`, `exp` and `cos` from `+`, `-`, `*`, `/`, comparisons, exact integer operations and the words of a `Float64Array` only; the five call sites use it (`rng.ts` Gaussian and exponential draws, `sensors.ts`, `stability.ts`; `(x - m) ** 2` is `(x - m) * (x - m)` in `evaluate/stats.ts`); a lint rule refuses the 22 transcendental `Math` members, `**` and `**=` in `engine/src` with a message that names ADR-0019. The goldens are regenerated once, float fields only, under the author's approval of 2026-10-30 ("결정적 수학 함수로 교체", the condition of ADR-0019). Base commit 0e0f408; nothing committed; no ADR, `package.json`, `docs/design/lab_ui.md`, parity golden or fixture touched.

**Port**: FreeBSD msun, src tag `release/14.1.0` (tag object 9699c03aaf9a), `lib/msun/src`: `e_log.c`, `e_exp.c`, `s_cos.c`, `k_cos.c`, `k_sin.c`, `e_rem_pio2.c`, and `rnint`, `irint` of `math_private.h`. Licence: the Sun Microsystems fdlibm notice ("Permission to use, copy, modify, and distribute this software is freely granted, provided that this notice is preserved"), preserved in the header with the variants of each file. Names, branch order and constants with their hexadecimal words follow the C source; the eight small cases of `e_rem_pio2.c` are folded into one function (`x - n*pio2_1` is the same double as the source's `x -+ |n|*pio2_1`). **Red, then green, per task**

Exact: log(1) = +0, exp(0) = exp(-0) = 1, cos(0) = cos(-0) = 1, and the special cases of the three sources (log of +-0, negatives, +Infinity, NaN; exp of +-Infinity, NaN, overflow, underflow; cos of +-Infinity, NaN). 47 decimal constants equal their hexadecimal words. Property test against Node v25.6.1's native functions: log 120,074 inputs (100,000 `1 - u` draws, 20,000 bit-pattern sweeps, 74 branch edges), 118,482 bit-identical, largest distance 1 ulp; cos 120,158 (`2 pi u`, |x| < 1.6e6, edges), 118,532 identical, 1 ulp; exp 120,094 (`-a / b` in whole seconds, [-746, 710), edges), 120,040 identical, 1 ulp.

- Section 15: items 1, 3 and 5 marked resolved with the author's quotes; items 2, 4, 6 and 7 marked ruled with the S3a review's rulings; items 8 and 9 added.

**The decision variant (section 9)**
- Target: the readjustment decision of experiment 2 (SC8; spec 6.2; `decideReadjust`, called by `runArm` at every readjustment tick of every reactor). The spec makes it per tick and reactor, not per sample, but each decision reads exactly one recorded row and names its sample. The error is the label error only, forced on a named sample (`challenge:pick_decision`, one keyed draw per eligible sample, minimum wins), reaching the decision through the container and hold time the arm reads by the id a row is filed under; the shown record is A or C without `system_issued_id`.
- The reader sees the decisions with the recorded row each read, never truth; a guess is a decision id: `hit` if it is among the decisions that read the planted pair or differ from the unplanted loop, `background` if it rests on a structural error or on SC8's own bias (open container A064, hold above A046), else `miss`. The reveal shows the affected decisions with record context against truth, and per architecture the label-error outcome (oracle `LABEL_CELLS`), the deciding flag and whether the decision at the tick is unchanged.
- Left out: the routine feed decision (the engine takes it on the true glucose in the truth world, no `feed_decision` event exists), `unit_rekey` and `duplicate_replay` as the error behind a decision (neither moves a decision), and a planted `clock_offset` (an analyzer-level setting in the spec, realised on every result of the analyzer, no sample-level target).

**Findings that shape the design (checked in the code)**: experiment 2 records the lab run once with no injector (`buildShared` in `closed_loop_shared.ts`), so no catalogue error reaches an arm today; `runArm` looks rows up by the physical sample id and keeps only counts, no decision log. The decision variant therefore needs a new engine mechanism (forced errors in the closed loop, a physical-id map for pH rows, a decision log). The note says so and proposes it as batch S3d. A194's note says experiment 2 decides the feed on the most recent recorded result, but spec 6.2 and the engine build only the pH loop.

**Gates** (run after the note was written)
- `uv run --frozen python scripts/check_docs.py --all` -> `549 files, 0 failure(s), 0 warning(s)`
- `uv run pytest -q` and `bash scripts/check_all.sh --manual` not run: the brief's Done line is the docs check, and the working tree holds the uncommitted engine files of the S3m batch.

**Parked**: the feed decision as a challenge target once a recorded-data feed rule exists; a decision variant for `unit_rekey` (needs a loop that reads rows by the recorded unit); SC8 in the Setup picker with its report.

**Open questions** (full text in section 15 of the note): (8) forced errors in the closed loop is a new engine mechanism and needs the orchestrator's go for batch S3d (spec clarification in `data_model.md` section 7 first); without it Step 4 builds the sample variant only and the author's answer 3 is met only in part; also the time of one SC8 seed through two loops, which has no budget. (9) The routine feed decision is left out, and the ledger text of A194 describes an experiment-2 feed loop that the spec and the engine do not build; for the research role and the orchestrator to reconcile.

The engine's transcendental functions come from `engine/src/xmath.ts`, a statement-for-statement port of FreeBSD msun (release/14.1.0, the Sun notice preserved), and a lint rule refuses the implementation-approximated `Math` members and the exponent operator in `engine/src` (ADR-0019, Proposed; decided by the author in chat). Executor: Opus 5.5 xhigh, by the rule of `prompts/README.md` (an invariant across several modules).
- Golden regeneration, the HITL gate: the author approved one regeneration in chat on the condition that only float fields change. The orchestrator's own field-by-field comparison of the old (HEAD) and new traces of seeds 1 to 3: 271 of 21,793 lines changed, 273 fields, all floats (`drift_before_pct` 181, `noise` 90, `value` 2), largest relative difference 8.18e-14, no structural difference (keys, ids, counts, integer times, order); the summaries changed in `log_hash` only. The parity traces and the trace oracle are unchanged.
- Orchestrator runs: `uv run pytest -q` 226 passed; `check_docs --all` 0 failures; `check_all --manual` PASS; `xmath.ts` lint-clean, its only `Math.log`, `exp` and `cos` are in comments. Orchestrator mutations: a cosine-kernel coefficient written with a different decimal that parses to the same double survived (equivalent mutant); the same coefficient moved by about 45 ulps -> 7 of 18 tests of `xmath.test.ts` and `golden_default.test.ts` fail; restored, `cmp` clean.
- Evidence from the executor, not rerun by the orchestrator: the strict log hashes of seeds 1 to 3 are equal in Node 25.6.1 (with the ADR-0016 flag), Chromium 156, Firefox 157 and WebKit 27.2 and equal to the regenerated goldens; the xmath bit patterns agree in the four engines while the native functions give four different results each. The lab's `engines` end-to-end check (S3b) makes this a standing test.
- Cost: the I12 median went from about 79 ms to about 119 ms (interleaved runs), within the 1 s budget. `cos` refuses arguments from about 2^20 pi/2 up (the large-argument reduction is not ported); the engine passes 2 pi u with u in [0, 1).
- Open, to the author: `ENGINE_VERSION` is still 0.1.0 while the bytes changed, against spec section 3 rule 3 ("same scenario, seed and engine_version gives a byte-identical event log"); a bump changes the `log_header` line of the goldens, a non-float field outside the approved condition.

## 2026-10-30 — Stop-hook batch marker (maintenance executor)

**What changed**: while `.claude/.batch-running` exists, the Stop hook (`scripts/check_all.sh` without `--manual` and without `--lab`) prints one line to stderr (the marker's content, i.e. batch name and start time, and that the gate is deferred to the orchestrator's `bash scripts/check_all.sh --manual`) and exits 0. It runs no check and writes neither `.claude/.last-check` nor clears `.claude/.dirty`, so the first turn after the marker is removed runs the gate. An empty, blank or unreadable marker still defers and the line says "empty or unreadable". `--manual` and `--lab` ignore the marker. The author's decision in chat on 2026-10-30: "묶음 진행 중에는 미룸 (Recommended)"; the orchestrator's `--manual` run before a commit and the pre-commit hook are as they were.

**Evidence**
- Red: 9 of the 12 new tests in `tests/test_check_all.py` failed before the change (the 3 `--manual`/`--lab` marker-ignoring cases already held and stay as guards).
- Green: `tests/test_check_all.py` 50 passed (38 before + 12 new); `uv run pytest -q` -> `284 passed, 76 deselected` (before 272).
- Mutation (scratch copy of `scripts/`, `tests/` and the config files, the real tree untouched, `diff -r` clean after): the hook writing `.last-check` while deferring -> 7 of 50 fail.
- `uv run --frozen python scripts/check_docs.py --all` -> `595 files, 0 failure(s), 0 warning(s)`; `bash scripts/check_all.sh --manual` -> `check_all --manual: PASS` (with the marker present, as it must).
- Smoke on the real tree: `echo '{}' | bash scripts/check_all.sh` -> the deferral line, exit 0, `.last-check` and `.dirty` modification times unchanged.

**Paths**: behaviour only, no structure-only change. Changed: `scripts/check_all.sh` (+9 -1: the marker branch after the `stop_hook_active` check, two header comment lines), `tests/test_check_all.py` (+101: a section "hook mode: the batch marker defers the gate"), `.gitignore` (+1 line and the comment of that block), `CLAUDE.md` (one sentence in Hooks), `prompts/README.md` ("Handing a prompt to an executor", new step 6 at the end so the numbered references to step 4 stay valid).

**Parked**: the deferral line goes to stderr with exit 0 as the brief says; Claude Code may show a Stop hook's stderr on exit 0 only in verbose mode, so "shows on every turn" is as visible as the harness makes it (a `systemMessage` JSON on stdout, as the `WARN` path uses, would be the louder form). A marker that is a dangling symlink does not defer (`-e` is false).

## 2026-10-30 — S3c part 1: the mixed-fleet design clarification (executor, uncommitted)

**Result**: `docs/design/simulation_spec.md` has a new subsection 4.5 "Mixed fleets (ADR-0015)" (six parts, 4.5.1 to 4.5.6) and one sentence in the section 4 intro; `docs/design/data_model.md` section 2 has one paragraph on the four attributes that may vary inside a run. Design only, no code, no schema, no scenario or profile file, no ADR, no `lab_ui.md` touched; base commit 4cbb1c9. The routing rule is marked "proposed, awaiting a ruling" in the heading, the status line and the text. No result of any simulation is stated.

**What 4.5 says**
- Today (read from `analysis.ts`, `run.ts`, `measure.ts`, `sampling.ts`, `scenario.ts`, `raw_setup.ts`): one analyzer profile per scenario; an analyzer is one instrument per route stage; every sample is one request with one visit per stage; one queue per group, strict head of line, lowest-index idle analyzer of the group; service time, method and transfer sit on the visit, so a pool cannot serve two profiles; the profile is read in four places outside the core (instruments and visits, `makeMeasure` by visit number, aliquot storage, raw layer).
- Fields: `bay_profiles` (one bioreactor profile id per bay, length = ceil(`reactor_count` / `bay_size`)) and `analyzer_profiles` (one analyzer profile id per analyzer, length = `analyzer_count`), top-level, optional, same names as the `RunSpec` fields; absent = the scenario profile everywhere. Instrument numbering stays stage by stage, so a repeated profile gives today's ids.
- Six load-time checks with messages in the style of the `bay_controllers` check: two lengths, unknown id, one group name in two profiles, a requested analyte no analyzer measures (requested = the route of `profiles.analyzer` plus the analytes the `stability` entries name), one analyte in two profiles (rule B only).
- Routing options A (pooled, lowest-index idle, whole route: needs a core and trace-schema change, so the author's SimPy parity must follow), B (split by analyte, one profile per analyte, disjoint only: plan built at load, core and trace untouched, no number) and C (fixed per bay before L3, habitual analyzer A034). Recommendation B. Blocked: a non-zero transfer between profiles (no ledger entry; A106 is inside the split profile) and any habit share other than A034's.
- Regression rule: lists absent = byte-identical default log (the three goldens); a list that spells out the default in every position gives the same log; no new draw purpose; `engine_version` is in the golden header, so a bump is a golden change that needs the author's recorded approval.
- Lab needs: the `RunSpec` lists, list rebuild when N, bay size or M change, a profile catalogue in `ready` (with the bundle as data), utilisation per analyzer profile in both windows (additive `by_profile`), shown once because the truth run is shared.

**The hand-typed decision case** (small SC8, technique probability 0.5, seed 3; the facts are read from the truth events and the technique draws, not from an arm): U01-D01-2 (closed, hold 1122 s) is the row that U01's decision at 201600 reads; the next draw is U04-D01-2 (open, hold 817 s). A: unplanted `readjust` on the row filed under U01-D01-2; planted `skip` (known bias) on the same value filed under U04-D01-2 with its container, and it is the only decision that differs. The opposite direction: U03-D01-2 (open) filed under U06-D01-2 (closed): `skip` becomes `readjust` onto the open sample's value. C: the planted loop equals the unplanted loop in every field. C without `system_issued_id`: the same decision differs as in A, with the neighbour's hold, 817 s (the run table holds one run for the shared id, written by the later `run_started`).

- a C decision reads the planted row (the injector told `system_issued_id` is off): 8 failed
- the sample pick keyed by the label (the neighbour's id): 1 failed

Every eligible sample as the plant over two seeds and the same three architectures: 1500 plants, no error, no failure of that property.

**Conflicts, clarifications and choices** (nothing guessed past)
1. Section 5 says `runArm` returns the log beside its counts. It does so on request (`{ decisions: true }`): returned always, the key would change every outcome hash and the condition of the S3a2 review is byte-identity. Section 18, item 5.

A recorded row is matched to its event without the analyzer, which is not a column of the row; the full-size run has results of two samples at one second on two analyzers (a pair of neighbours can be filed under one id), told apart by the value. ### Part 2 — the design revised to the author's rulings (executor, uncommitted)

**Result**: `docs/design/simulation_spec.md` section 11.8 is rewritten to the rulings of 2026-10-30 (D1 equipment included, D3 mycoplasma included, D4 the lag contains the ramp; D2 and D5 as recommended; the trace-fixture regeneration approved for the flag-list entries and the checksum only). `docs/design/data_model.md` section 15 gains the `equipment` entity, the `used_in` relation, the `equipment_use` event kind, the equipment states, K19, the extended VR-021 and a rewritten 15.6; section 14 question 8 mentions equipment. Nothing else touched: no code, ledger, ADR, prompt, `docs/progress.md`, `data_model` section 7 or `lab_ui.md`. `check_docs --all`: 688 files, 0 failures. Base is the tree as part 1 left it.

**Equipment, in three lines.** (1) A new entity `equipment` (one shared item used by several units; key `equipment_id`, `name`, `state`), not a `material_lot` kind: it is not consumed, has no quantity or `prepared_at`, and its states (in service, on hold, quarantined) differ from a lot's; a unit's own equipment is the unit and has no row. (2) A new relation `used_in` (equipment item to the `equipment_use` process event that used it), the equipment counterpart of `consumed_in`; an origin item has a hidden `contaminated_at` and each unit that uses it from then on gets its onset at the use instant. (3) Direction 3 reaches siblings through it in the same one-hop step (back along `consumed_in`, `seeded_from` and `used_in`, forward to the other batches), not a new direction, because A149 and 11.4 already list equipment beside the lots in direction 3. Flags: the lot-link flag decides whether the `equipment` rows and `used_in` edges are stored; no other flag touches equipment beyond what it does for lots. A unit's own equipment is an origin kind with a scenario onset and no sibling.

**Mycoplasma inputs and the horizon.** Required scenario inputs with no default, printed with every result: N1 DO rise ramp, N2 plateau delay, N3 growth multiplier after it, N4 viability-fall delay, N5 death multiplier after it, N6 nucleic-acid target covers the organism, N7 and N8 the bioburden and nucleic-acid sample volumes. Tests: nucleic-acid 1 d (A146), indicator cells 8 d (A145), culture 28 d (A144). The engine gains one generic scripted multiplier moment (growth and death multipliers set at an instant, as the temperature shift does). Horizon: the scenario `horizon_days` must contain the last result and the loader refuses a shorter one (not "left open"), so no result is ever open at the end and the metrics never see one; the mycoplasma scenario needs 31 d (culture result at 727 h in the fixture). SC12's mycoplasma variant is `sc12_mycoplasma`: the medium-lot case with the mycoplasma type, run with the nucleic-acid target covering and not covering.

**Plainly impossible or not covered.** Mycoplasma ammonia, arginine, off-gas CO2 and base demand: the engine has no such analyte or tag and the ledger has no magnitude, so they are not built; the mycoplasma signal in S3 is the DO rise and the growth and viability effects. The mycoplasma timings of A138 come from one species in one line in perfusion and are applied to the fed-batch default as a proxy. Equipment has no rate, no list of shared items and no sharing topology in the ledger; the topology is a required input (A177 widened) and no rate is modelled. A lag at the low end of the A139 range (4 h) is shorter than the default ramp (6 h), so a sweep of the true lag below 6 h must set a shorter ramp; the sweep of the assumed lag is unaffected.

**The lag change (D4) and direction 1.** The lag now runs from the onset to `signal_at` (the instant the true DO value reaches its alarm limit, A197) and the ramp ends there; the loader refuses a ramp longer than the lag. With the assumed lag at the default of the type the walk-back estimate is the true onset, so direction 1 no longer misses the first samples by construction (the earlier reading put the estimate 6 h late). Direction 1 loses recall when the assumed lag is shorter than the true lag and precision when it is longer; the sweep shows it. FX12a is retyped on the new meaning (onset 5 h, `signal_at` 17 h, decision 19 h).

**Revised batches** (11.8.5): 1 pins and the ninth flag (trace fixture limited to the flag-list entries and the checksum; any other difference stops the batch); 2 truth side: bacterial, lots and equipment; 3 recorder (a structure-only `ResultRow.value` widening first); 4 investigation; 5 mycoplasma (the profile, the DO rise, the multiplier moment, the three tests, the horizon check, FX12m end to end); 6 evaluator and SC12 (five scenarios, mutation checks, `sim-adversary`). The lab: dictionary entries and the `flags` check after batch 1; the Worker function, the screen, `sc12`, the Pages workflow after batch 6.

**Fixtures typed by hand**: FX12a (clean sibling through a shared medium lot, retyped), FX12e (equipment origin, an affected sibling that signals 4 h later: direction 3 reaches TL-1 and B-U02 through `used_in` in C only, C-L contains B-U02 only at its own signal), FX12m (mycoplasma: times of the three tests, the nucleic-acid covering and not covering, the horizon cell 30 d refused and 31 d accepted). FX12b of part 1 is dropped: FX12e covers the affected sibling.

**New decision for the author (D6)**: which flag covers equipment links. ADR-0017 defines the ninth flag for lot links, and a further flag needs its own ADR (ADR-0005). Options: (a) the ninth flag covers lot and equipment links (an amendment of the Proposed text of ADR-0017, which the orchestrator writes and the author re-confirms); (b) a tenth flag `equipment_links` with its own ADR. Recommendation (a). The plan assumes (a); under (b) batch 1 adds the second flag the same way.

#### Ledger changes the rulings need (for the orchestrator to apply; the ledger is not edited here)

Changes to existing entries (the text to replace or add):

- **A139** `bacterial_onset_to_signal_lag_h` (value 12, range 4 to 36, grade A, sources unchanged). Prepend to the notes: "Meaning, by the author's ruling of 2026-10-30: the lag is the time from the onset of contamination to the first process signal, the instant the true DO value reaches its low alarm limit (A197). The fall of DO and pH (A141) takes place inside the lag, in its last hours, so a scenario whose lag is shorter than its ramp is refused at load, and a lag at the low end of the range needs a ramp no longer than it. The walk-back estimates the onset as the first signal minus an assumed lag whose default is this one; when the assumed lag equals the true lag the estimate is the true onset." The rest of the notes stays.
- **A141** `bacterial_signal_magnitude_placeholder`. New default: "DO falls from its set point to its low alarm limit (A197) over the last 6 h of the lag and reaches it at the first signal; pH falls 0.3 below set point over the same hours before base addition catches up; the 6 h is a scenario knob and must not exceed the lag (A139)". Append to the notes: "Changed by the author's ruling of 2026-10-30: the earlier text put the 6 h after the lag; it now lies inside it. Range, grade and sources unchanged."
- **A138** `mycoplasma_signal_lag_days` (value 2, range 2 to 6, grade S, S063). Append to the notes: "Used as the time from onset to the first process signal, the instant the true DO value reaches its high alarm limit (A197), the same meaning as A139; the rise of DO takes place inside the lag over the hours of the scenario input N1. The growth plateau and the viability fall of the same source (2 to 4 d and after 3 to 6 d) are the ranges of N2 and N4. One species, one line, perfusion; applied to the fed-batch default as a proxy."
- **A177** `shared_lot_topology`. New name `shared_lot_and_equipment_topology`; default "no default; a scenario input giving, for each seed lot, media lot, feed lot and shared equipment item, which reactors draw from or use it and when"; unit "reactors per lot or item"; range "1 to the number of reactors (A001)". Append to the notes: "Equipment is added by the author's ruling of 2026-10-30. A149 names equipment among the things that tie reactors together; no source gives which items are shared or by how many reactors. The topology is a map from lot or item to units and instants, set per scenario and printed with every result."
- **A197** `process_alarm_schedule_default`. Append to the notes: "Also used by the break-out events: the DO low alarm (set point minus 10 % air saturation) is the first signal of a bacterial contamination and the DO high alarm (plus 10 %) that of a mycoplasma contamination (spec 11.8, rule S1). No alarm event is rendered by this use."
- **A158** `profile_contamination_detection`: add N1 to N8 to the notes as scenario inputs of the bundle (not components: they have no default). Optional.

New entries (all `grade: A`, `default: no default; a scenario input printed with every result`, ids assigned by the orchestrator):

| Proposed id | name | unit | range the scenario carries | sources | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| N1 | `mycoplasma_do_rise_ramp_h` | h | above 0 and at most the lag (A138) | S063 (direction: DO and gas-mix trends were indicators from about 2 d) | Length of the DO rise that ends at the high alarm at the first signal. No magnitude or shape in the source. |
| N2 | `mycoplasma_plateau_delay_d` | d | plausible 2 to 4 (`range_grade` S) | S063 | Delay from onset to the start of the growth plateau; the range is the one S063 reports (one species, one line, perfusion). |
| N3 | `mycoplasma_growth_multiplier_after_plateau` | factor | 0 to 1 (logical bounds; 0 is a full plateau) | S063 (qualitative) | Multiplier on the growth rate from the plateau instant. No magnitude in any source read. |
| N4 | `mycoplasma_viability_fall_delay_d` | d | plausible 3 to 6 (`range_grade` S) | S063 | Delay from onset to the start of the viability fall. |
| N5 | `mycoplasma_death_multiplier_after_fall` | factor | 1 or more (logical bound; no upper end sourced) | S063 (qualitative) | Multiplier on the death rate from the fall instant. No magnitude in any source read. |
| N6 | `nat_target_covers_organism` | flag | 0 or 1 | S058, S060 (a nucleic-acid test finds only what its target covers; A146) | Whether the nucleic-acid test reports the organism as `detected`. |
| N7 | `bioburden_sample_volume_ml` | mL | above 0 | none found (A142, A148 notes) | Volume of the bioburden draw. |
| N8 | `nat_sample_volume_ml` | mL | above 0 | none found (A146 note) | Volume of the nucleic-acid draw. |
| N9 | `hold_release_delay_h` | h | at least 0 | S065, S058 (as A176) | Delay from the last negative result to the release of a hold; A176 covers the delay to a hold after a positive result only. Until it exists S3 reuses the second A176 delay and prints it as assumed. |

Impossible without a source, stated rather than designed around: mycoplasma ammonia, arginine, off-gas CO2 and base demand (no analyte or tag in the engine, no magnitude); a rate of equipment contamination and the list of shared items (no source); a viral signal lag (11.7).

**Decisions to confirm (the brief did not settle them)**
1. (Settled by the orchestrator in chat after the first report: the profile goes through the one loader.) `schemas/profile.schema.json` gains the kind `breakout` and the optional fields `contamination_type`, `alarm_signal`, `signals`, `tests`, `scenario_inputs` (additive; `profile.schema.d.ts` regenerated, `ProfileKind` widens by itself); `loadScenario` keeps its two arguments and loads the bacterial profile with `loadProfile(lookup("breakout", id), "breakout")`; a lookup that answers with another kind or a file the schema or the reader refuses is reported as "breakout profile <id> could not be loaded (...)". The data test validates `bacterial.json` against the schema.
2. A consumption or use of the origin item exactly at the first decision is refused (the rule says "after"; equality would let a unit become affected at the instant of the draws). Spec C7.
3. A consumption or use at or after the end a unit really has leaves no record (a stop or an earlier harvest), as a feed after the stop does not happen.
4. The confirming volume counts against the cap of its own draw only. Later routine draws of a unit that keeps running (the sibling that signals later) do not see it, because `sampling.ts` is outside this brief. It matters only on vessels small enough to be near the cap; there the confirming draw is skipped anyway (the micro-scale test). Spec C5.
5. Lot `kind`, `prepared_at` and the equipment `name` are not in the event stream; they are plain data of the topology that batch 3 passes to the recorder (data_model 15.6, clarification). The hidden `contaminated_at` is not part of that data.
6. The assumed lag, the release delay (A227) and the mycoplasma inputs (A219 to A224, A226) are not in the block; batches 4 and 5 add them. `positive_to_hold` is required and read by nothing yet.

**Sizes**: the new source files are 24 to 166 lines; `engine/src/process.ts` is 316 lines (301 at the base, so the 300 line target was missed before this batch). A split of `processConfig` (the unit-reading block, about 55 lines) into its own file would bring it under; it is a structure-only step of its own and was not done here.

**Paths.** Structure only (tests green before and after): `engine/src/process.ts` (the three extracted functions), `engine/tests/breakout/inoculation.test.ts`; tidy: `engine/src/record/capture.ts`, `engine/src/experiments/assembly/config.ts` (one comment), `schemas/scenario.schema.json` (description) and the generated `engine/src/generated/scenario.schema.d.ts`. Behaviour: `schemas/scenario.schema.json` (`breakout`), `schemas/event.schema.json`, `schemas/profile.schema.json` (kind `breakout` and its fields), `engine/src/generated/{event,scenario,profile}.schema.d.ts`, `engine/src/breakout/*`, `engine/src/{scenario,run,process,observe}.ts`, `eslint.config.js`, `scenarios/profiles/breakout/bacterial.json`, `engine/tests/breakout/*` (`support.ts` and ten test files besides the structure-only pin), `docs/design/simulation_spec.md` (11.8.8), `docs/design/data_model.md` (15.6), this entry.

**Parked**: split `process.ts` as above; counting a confirming draw against the later routine draws of the same unit once `sampling.ts` may change; a viral or fungal profile needs no code (data only), a mycoplasma profile needs batch 5.


**Rework (the one round, at the orchestrator's chat ruling)**: (R1) one loader for profiles. `schemas/profile.schema.json` gains the kind `breakout` and the optional fields `contamination_type`, `alarm_signal`, `signals`, `tests`, `scenario_inputs` (additive, `profile.schema.d.ts` regenerated); `loadScenario` has two arguments again and loads `scenarios/profiles/breakout/bacterial.json` with `loadProfile(lookup("breakout", id), "breakout")`; the third argument and the separate parse are gone. `engine/src/breakout/profile.ts` keeps only what a schema cannot say: that the break-out fields are present for the kind, that the names `signals` and `tests` use resolve to a param or a scenario input, that the alarm signal is one of the signals and moves, and the units the engine expects (the kind check is the loader's). Tests: the two that no longer applied (no lookup) became two that the one loader and the reader refuse a wrong-kind answer and an unknown volume name, and the data test validates the file against the schema (130 tests in `engine/tests/breakout`); the pins stayed green. (R2) the comment "eight runs" in `engine/src/experiments/assembly/config.ts` line 10 now says ten. Rulings written into the spec: 11.8.2 rule S4 says "at or after" the first decision instant, rule S3 states the simplification that a confirming draw is not counted against later routine draws. `engine/tests/fleet.test.ts` (approved): two parameter types became `ProfileKind` (a widened kind made `tsc` fail there); nothing else in the file. Paths added by the rework: `schemas/profile.schema.json`, `engine/src/generated/profile.schema.d.ts`, `engine/src/experiments/assembly/config.ts`, `engine/tests/fleet.test.ts`.

2. **FX12a reach of B and C-L.** The table said "no"; B and C-L have `equipment_links` on (ADR-0020), and S8 says direction 3 is unreachable only when both flags are off. Built by S8, the cell corrected (C19).
3. **The events.** The instant of the quarantine is stored nowhere (a hold has `released_at` for its end), so `investigate()` returns `events` (`hold_placed`, `hold_quarantined`, `hold_released`, `hold_rejected`, `result_reflagged`, `deviation_closed`), the 11.4 events of the per-architecture step with two names added (C27).
4. **Which sample a hold waits for** (C22): the item's own batch, else the signalling unit. FX12a needs the first (the clean sibling is released by its own negative); a lot has no batch, so it needs the second.
5. **Closing** (C24): at the later of the last confirming result and the last end of a hold. Not in S9; the deviation machine of 15.2 needs an instant.

No file of `engine/src/breakout/` passes 300 lines (the longest, `config.ts`, has 186).

**Decisions to confirm (the brief or the spec did not settle them)**
1. **The scripted factor multiplies the shift's multiplier (C28).** Rule S13 says the multiplier "takes the value N3" and, in the same rule, that a multiplier of 1 leaves the log byte-identical. After the temperature shift both hold only if the factor multiplies; before it they read the same. Built as a product; the mutation (2) shows the test that guards it.
2. **The mycoplasma effects barely reach the signalling unit with the default lag (C29).** The unit stops at `signal_at` plus d1, and N2 and N4 count from the onset: with the lag of 2 d (A138) and N2 in 2 to 4 d (A220) the plateau comes at or after the signal, and the viability fall (N4, 3 to 6 d, A222) never comes before the stop. Rule S13 is built as written and works (a lag of 96 h shows both moments); FX12m does not depend on it. If the author wants the host effects visible on the signalling unit by default, the design needs a longer d1 or a different reference instant for the delays; that is a design ruling and not made here.
3. **The A148 volumes (C32).** "10.5 mL culture plus 1 mL indicator cell, 11.5 mL" is read as the volume of the culture test and of the indicator-cell test, so the two together are 11.5 mL (11.4 and A148 say the set is 77 to 115 percent of a micro vessel, which needs 11.5 and not 23). The other reading, 11.5 mL for each test, would double the set.
4. **Which inputs the profile requires (C33).** N1 to N6 and N8. N7 (A225) belongs to the bacterial profile and is refused here; N9 (A227) is read by no step of the truth run, the investigation uses d2 for a release (C23), so it is not in the block. FX12m's "N1 to N8" is read that way.
5. **`ramp_input` (C31).** A profile with no default ramp names the input that holds it, and the block's `ramp` is refused for it. The alternative, the block's `ramp` made required, would have left A219 outside the declared inputs. A schema field of the profile (additive).
6. **`Reactor.factors` is a new field** of the process output (empty without a moment); nothing serialises a reactor, and the hash pins did not move.
7. **Dictionary entries**: `result.hold_id`, and `placed_by` and `reason` on the audit event of a flag, are `nullable: false` (absent, not null, elsewhere; VR-022 needs `audit_trail`, so the person and the reason are always stored on a flag). `quality_hold` is a dataset member (it left `other`).

**Found outside the allowed paths, not touched**
- `engine/src/export/rows.ts` still throws on a confirming result (no canonical unit for the test), now also for the three mycoplasma tests; `engine/src/evaluate/truth.ts` (`truthFrom`) still counts a skipped confirming draw as a request. Both are carried from batches 3 and 4 and belong to the export and the evaluator batch.
- `engine/src/process.ts` is 343 lines (316 at the base, 301 before batch 2): the split of `processConfig` into its own file, parked since batch 2, would bring it under 300.

**Paths.** Structure only (tests green before and after): `engine/src/process.ts` (`multOf`, `shiftOn`). Behaviour: `engine/src/process.ts`, `engine/src/breakout/{profile,config,plan,events,script}.ts`, `schemas/profile.schema.json`, `schemas/scenario.schema.json`, `engine/src/generated/{profile,scenario}.schema.d.ts`, `scenarios/profiles/breakout/mycoplasma.json`, `docs/design/data_dictionary.yaml`, `engine/tests/support/{dictionary_cli,fx12m_streams}.ts`, `tests/test_data_dictionary.py`, `engine/tests/breakout/{support_m,fx12m_times,mycoplasma_loader,mycoplasma_data,mycoplasma_determinism,mycoplasma_recorded,multiplier}.ts` (`.test.ts` except `support_m.ts`), `engine/tests/record/investigate_fx12m.test.ts`, `docs/design/simulation_spec.md` (11.8.11), `docs/design/data_model.md` (15.6), this entry.
