**Decisions to confirm (the brief or the spec did not settle them)**
1. **The export of a confirming result (follow-up 3).** The brief says "it writes the outcome as the design says". The export table has no outcome column, `value` and `canonical_unit` are required, `tests/test_data_dictionary.py` requires the dictionary entity `export_result` to equal `EXPORT_FIELDS`, and 11.8.1 says the break-out export package is not built in S3. I asked the orchestrator (X: leave the result out; Y: an optional `outcome` column in break-out exports only); the answer after my report was X, which is what is built (one condition in `rows.ts`, one test file) and is now one sentence in the 11.8.1 row of the export package. Y would be a further batch: `columns.ts`, `package.ts`, `export_result` in the dictionary, its CLI and `tests/test_data_dictionary.py`.
2. **Items are scored by the id the record holds them under (C37).** A result is `<request>/<analyte>` with the request mapped through `recordedId`, as `lookupAll` reads it; two samples that a label error gives one id are one item. A consequence: the false-alarm denominator of direction 3 differs by a few items between architectures that realise label errors (A and C without `system_issued_id`) and those that do not. The alternative, physical identity, would need the inverse of the key and is ambiguous where two rows share an id.
3. **The universe is the topology (C35).** "All lots and equipment items of the run" is read as the lots and items the block declares (`Truth.breakout`), not only those some event names. In the five scenarios every declared item is used, so the two readings give the same numbers here.
4. **A release at the instant of the hold stands first (C39),** by C25 (the review of an instant runs before the investigation); strictly "before" would flip it. Only a hand-built case tells them apart.
5. **A result the walk-back cannot see at the decision (C39).** A sample drawn shortly before the stop has its result after the decision instant; it is in `T`, never held, counts as released before containment and is incomplete, and recall pays. I met it on one seed of the medium-lot case (recall 1 of 7: a sample drawn 15 min before the stop) while checking that a low spread was not a bug. This is how rules S8 and S10 read together; if the author wants those samples covered, the investigation would need a second pass, which is a design question.
6. **The default assumed lag is the lag the scenario gives the event (C41),** the type's own when the block gives none. The seed-lot scenario sets the lag to 36 h (the top of the range of A139), because with 12 h a contaminated seed signals before the first routine draw (day 1), no routine result falls in `T` and direction 1 would have nothing but the batch; with the assumed lag at the true 36 h the estimate is the true onset, and the sweep (12 h, 4 h) shows the loss.
7. **Scenario inputs are test design choices, printed as inputs:** d1 2 h and d2 4 h (A176, as the fixtures), the bioburden volume 2 mL (A225), the mycoplasma inputs (ramp 12 h, plateau delay 2 d, growth factor 0.5, viability-fall delay 3 d, death factor 2, nucleic-acid volume 1 mL), the d1 sweep values 6 h and 12 h (A176 has no range). N2 and N4 are at the low end of the plausible ranges of S063, which puts the host effects at or after the stop of the signalling unit (C29, as ruled). The mycoplasma horizon is 33 d (the culture result is 28 d after the draw at 102 h, A144).
8. **`-CONF` is how `truth.ts` knows a confirming sample (C36).** The event `draw_skipped_volume_budget` has no purpose field and `schemas/event.schema.json` is outside the allowed paths; a test pins the suffix against the ids the engine produces.
9. **The evaluator imports the types of the walk-back** (`record/investigate/types.ts`, types only); the allow-list of Guard 6 in `gaming_guards.test.ts` gained that one entry.
10. **Not built, by design:** direction 2 and the metric draws beyond the port limit (S4); the Break-out screen and the Worker-facing function (Step 6).

**Found outside the allowed paths, not touched**: nothing new. `engine/src/process.ts` is still 343 lines (parked in batch 5).

**Spec clarifications, listed.** `docs/design/simulation_spec.md`: 11.8.1 (one sentence in the row of the export package: the export skips confirming results until the break-out export is built), 11.8.7 (the intro line gains the date of the C29 ruling; a new row "C29") and a new section 11.8.12 with C35 (wiring), C36 (the skipped confirming draw), C37 (what an item is), C38 (time to containment and what is in hand), C39 (released before containment, completeness, the result the walk-back cannot see), C40 (the report and the C29 sentence), C41 (the experiment). `data_model.md` and the data dictionary are unchanged: the batch adds no table, column or transition.

**Paths.** Structure only: none (no step of this batch was a structure-only change). Behaviour: `engine/src/evaluate/{breakout_truth,breakout_metrics,truth}.ts`, `engine/src/experiments/assembly/{breakout,breakout_config,breakout_inputs,breakout_report,from_run,run_seed,open_loop}.ts`, `engine/src/export/rows.ts`, `schemas/breakout_report.schema.json`, `engine/src/generated/breakout_report.schema.d.ts` (generator only), `scenarios/sc12_{medium_lot,seed_lot,sampling,equipment,mycoplasma}.json`, `scenarios/experiments/sc12.json`, new tests `engine/tests/{sc12,sc12_experiment}.test.ts`, `engine/tests/assembly/{breakout_follow_ups,breakout_config}.test.ts`, `engine/tests/evaluate/breakout_truth.test.ts`, `engine/tests/export/export_breakout.test.ts`, new support `engine/tests/support/{breakout_truth_setup,sc12,sc12_cli}.ts`, three existing test files changed by one entry each (`engine/tests/scenarios_data.test.ts`: the five scenario names; `engine/tests/evaluate/gaming_guards.test.ts`: the allow-list; `engine/tests/support/eval_schemas.ts`: the new schema), `docs/design/simulation_spec.md`, this entry.

The throwaway-ledger tests: a ledger where one source is a snippet makes `public` false, the same ledger with that source `main-reread` makes it true; a copy of the real ledgers in which the sources behind the screen read `main-reread` makes the real screen public, and the real ledgers are not touched. Today `public` is false; the sources behind the screen below main-reread are S049, S058 to S066, S068, S069, S071 to S073, S082, S083, S099 (S049 behind A177, S099 behind A197: the range S057 to S083 of the brief is a little short). A public build is `npm run build:lab` with no `VITE_LAB_LOCAL`; a local one is `VITE_LAB_LOCAL=1` (the QA builds it so). In the public build the navigation has no link, `#/breakout` answers with the refusal, `ready.breakout` is `null` and the Worker bundles none of the five scenarios, two profiles or the config (measured: the public Worker chunk holds none of their names; it did hold them while the rule read a JSON property, which the bundler does not fold, hence the generated TypeScript constant).

Budgets, the project's own measurements in headless Chromium on this machine, over the assembled public pages: infographic 37.6 KB gzip (limit 150 KB); lab first load 135.2 KB gzip over 5 files (limit 300 KB; the local build that `qa_lab.py` serves is 139.0 KB); a run of seed 1 in the Worker, median 118 ms of 5 (limit 1000 ms). The public build refuses the Break-out screen (`breakout_public` in the lab QA, `lab_public` on the folder): no link, no `ready.breakout`, none of the SC12 files in the Worker.

**Process notes.** Mutations, each reverted: 13 of the workflow (a push trigger, default true, a tag instead of a SHA, a SHA without its release comment, another SHA, `path: site`, hidden files dropped, deploy always, write permission, `--ci` dropped, the secret not passed, Node version hard-coded, a `git push` step) all caught; 8 of the assembly script (the environment kept, the whole infographic folder copied, no wipe, no `.nojekyll`, wipe before the check, `reference.json` not required, a failed build ignored, assembly before the build) all caught; 5 of the assembled folder run through `qa_site.py` (root-absolute link to the lab, root-absolute link back, `build.py` in the folder, `reference.json` missing, a non-empty `.nojekyll`) all caught. One mutation (the order swap, which keeps the file size) left a stale `.pyc` in `scripts/__pycache__` that made the next full pytest run fail one test; the source was never wrong, the cache file was deleted and the run repeated green.

**Finds and decisions to confirm**
1. **A secret that scans for nothing.** `GUARDRAIL_TERMS` set but blank or all comments gave an empty list, and `check_docs.py --all` passed. Red first, three tests in `tests/test_check_docs.py`; `--ci` now fails on an empty list (a `check_docs.py` change of one condition, local runs without `--ci` unchanged). A failing `--all --ci` prints the matched terms (the existing message). The runner masks the secret's value in its logs; I could not verify here that it masks every line of a multi-line secret, so a log line with a term is not ruled out. If the author wants that excluded, the message should print counts and file names instead (a change to `docs_rules.py` that this batch did not make). The list has no capital letters, so the lowercase print equals the secret. The secret should hold the terms only, without the comment lines.
2. **The upload action drops dot files.** `upload-pages-artifact` (its `action.yml` at v5.0.0) excludes everything that starts with a dot unless `include-hidden-files: true`, so `.nojekyll` would have been left out silently; the workflow sets it and a test pins it. (Whether a site deployed from an Actions artifact would run Jekyll at all was not verified here; the file is what the design asks for and is harmless.)
3. **Before the first run**, the author: sets Pages to "GitHub Actions", creates the secret, and starts the workflow with `deploy` false first (the artifact then expires after the default one day; nothing is published). Node comes from `.nvmrc` (25, the newest 25.x on the runner, local 25.6.1); the build refuses to produce a wrong lab, because `lab_reference_cli.ts` stops when its hashes differ from the goldens.
4. **Language is not carried by the links.** The two pages share an origin on Pages but store the language under `sll.lang` (infographic) and `lab.lang` (lab); a reader who chose Korean on one lands on the other in English. The author's reference of 2026-11-01 (a walkable three.js space with a third-person avatar, click to walk, drag to look, a top HUD and a bottom action bar) is translated into feel, not look; no file, model, texture, sound or text of it is used. The repository was never edited by the spike (the engine was imported by path); `git status` shows the one new file and this file.

**The note** (sections): 1 what the plant is and the rules it keeps; 2 the feel taken from the reference, and the asset policy (procedural, made for the project); 3 the scene (layout from data for N by M, the objects, three layers: floor as the simulation's own view, truth overlay, one record hall per architecture; camera and navigation); 4 from event log to motion (the scene plan, a stateless scene as a function of the clock, every event type to what the reader sees); 5 operator actions (plain data, two classes, keys and order, recompute and resume, refusals, the proof of equality with a hand-written scenario, the engine work); 6 the Worker protocol additions; 7 the S3 screens as drill-downs; 8 budgets and the spike; 9 fallbacks and access; 10 QA; 11 build plan P1 to P9; 12 Open.

**Operator actions proposed**: first build `add_feed`, `move_draw`, `analyzer_down`, `set_flag`, `set_capture_path`; with the break-out scenes `place_contamination` (refused in the public build); optional, because the engine has them, `raise_alarm`, `change_lot`, `set_labelling`. Plant actions change the truth run and act on the future of their instant only; lab settings re-make the record for the whole run and leave the truth hash unchanged. Refused: rewriting the past, writing a value, anything that shows a hidden field, acting on a recorded result (closes the loop), planting an error, a break-out action in a public build, any action while a challenge is open. `move_draw` is the one action the engine lacks (a new scripted event type: spec first, additive schema, one change in `sampling.ts`); the daily feed-rule shift per reactor is not offered because the rule is a whole-run setting.

**The spike** (three.js 0.186.1, published 2026-09-24, the newest release at least two weeks old on 2026-11-01; Vite 8.3.5; headless Chromium 156.0.8078.4, software WebGL over SwiftShader, 1280 by 720; the default scenario's 24 reactors in two bays, 2 analyzers, staff, a bench, three record shelves; the repository's engine in a Web Worker, one seed, records A, B and C; `move_draw` emulated by one extra line in a copy of `sampling.ts` swapped in the scratch bundle)
- Plant chunk 136,113 B gzip (`gzip -c`; 547,028 B raw); three.js's renderer alone in a trivial scene 129,639 B; loader 2,053 B; Worker 57,311 B (the lab surface plus a track builder). A first load that fetched the plant chunk would be about 270 KB of 300 KB, so the plant is a lazy chunk behind an entry screen.
- Frame interval, samples moving, median and 95th percentile over three repeats: 33.4 and 50.1 ms, 33.4 and 50.1 ms, 49.9 and 50.1 ms (20 to 30 frames a second; the same with the scene static, so the cost is software rasterising, not the update; JS update 0.0 ms median). At 960 by 540: 33.2 and 33.4 ms. At 390 by 844: 16.7 and 16.7 ms.
- Action round trip, click to first new frame: a scripted feed median 299.8 ms, 95th percentile 303.5 ms (cold 375.9 ms); a moved draw median 292.7 ms, 95th percentile 303.9 ms (cold 302.4 ms). Worker about 230 ms (truth 132, three records 90 to 95), message 0.3 ms, page 66 ms to the first frame.
- Causality: the events before the action's instant were identical to the run without it for both actions (3,458 and 3,277 events); one hash in ten repeats of each; undo returned to the base hash. With WebGL disabled the loader showed the text alternative and never requested the plant chunk. Plan 28,112 bytes.
- In Node (the spike's set-up, one seed, three records): 24 reactors 0.26 s, 50 reactors 0.77 s, 100 reactors 3.0 s (records grow faster than the truth run), hence a progressive answer for larger fleets.
- Not measured: a real GPU, Firefox and WebKit, authored assets, sound.

**Budgets proposed** (the author sets them when accepting ADR-0021): first load unchanged at most 300 KB gzip; plant chunk at most 200 KB gzip for code (assets counted apart, 0 in version one); frame interval in software WebGL at 1280 by 720 median at most 50 ms and 95th percentile at most 80 ms; action round trip of the default scenario median at most 600 ms and 95th percentile at most 1 s.
